- [x] **3.6.14** Refactor `server.Application/UseCases/TimeEntries/Upsert/UpsertTimeEntryUseCase.cs` to the dual-shape contract:
  - **Dispatch by role × payload shape** (the use case owns ALL of these — the validator is role-independent and does not enforce mutual exclusion of `Action` vs `Segments`). Run after authentication so the BranchUser.Role is known. Check in this order so each combination produces a single, role-appropriate error:
    - Member caller with `request.Segments != null` → 400 `TIMEENTRY_MEMBER_SHOULD_NOT_SEND_SEGMENTS` (covers both "Member with Segments only" and "Member with both Action and Segments").
    - Member caller with `request.Action == null` → 400 `TIMEENTRY_MEMBER_TAP_ACTION_REQUIRED` (covers "Member with neither").
    - Manager/Admin caller with `request.Action != null` → 400 `TIMEENTRY_ADMIN_SHOULD_NOT_SEND_TAP_ACTION` (covers both "Admin with Action only" and "Admin with both").
    - Manager/Admin caller with `request.Segments == null` → 400 `TIMEENTRY_ADMIN_REQUIRES_SEGMENTS` (covers "Admin with neither").
  - **Member tap branch** (`request.Action` set, `request.Segments` null):
    - Status must be `Present`; otherwise → 400 `TIMEENTRY_NON_PRESENT_REJECTS_SEGMENTS` (Member taps only support Present; Member non-Present writes were already blocked by the permission guard, this is defense in depth).
    - Load existing `TimeEntry` tracked with active segments by `(BranchId, OperatorId, Date)`.
    - Capture `now = branchClock.LocalBusinessDateTime(branchClock.UtcNow())` once.
    - Dispatch on `request.Action`:
      - `Open`:
        - If no `TimeEntry` exists → create one and insert a new `TimeEntrySegment { ClockIn = now, ClockOut = null }`.
        - Else if any active segment has `ClockOut == null` → **idempotent no-op**: do not insert, do not stamp parent audit, return current state (the caller's previous `Open` already took effect). The persisted `TimeEntry.UpdatedAt` does NOT advance on idempotent retry.
        - Else (all segments closed) → insert a new open segment with `ClockIn = now`.
      - `Close`:
        - If `TimeEntry` exists with an active open segment → set that segment's `ClockOut = now`. Stamp `segment.UpdatedAt`/`UpdatedByUserId` and parent audit.
        - Else if TimeEntry exists but all segments are already closed → idempotent no-op: return current state without persisting anything.
        - Else if no TimeEntry exists → 404 TIMEENTRY_NOT_FOUND (or 400 TIMEENTRY_CLOSE_REQUIRES_EXISTING_ENTRY, if you prefer a validation error).
    - On any non-no-op branch, run the per-segment guards on the resulting state (overlap, multiple-open, day-bounds, ClockOut > ClockIn). Day-bounds: `segment.ClockIn ∈ [parent.Date, parent.Date + 1 day)`, `segment.ClockOut > segment.ClockIn`, span ≤ 24h. The Member flow should not violate these by construction, but the guard catches edge cases (e.g., a tap at 00:01 of the next branch-local day on an entry whose `Date` hasn't rolled over yet, or a `Close` whose now is more than 24h after the open segment's `ClockIn`).
  - **Admin snapshot branch** (`request.Segments != null`, `request.Action == null`):
    - Status-relative rules: non-Present requires empty `Segments` (`TIMEENTRY_NON_PRESENT_REJECTS_SEGMENTS`); status change from `Present` to non-Present while persisted active segments exist (`TIMEENTRY_STATUS_CHANGE_REQUIRES_SEGMENT_CLEANUP`).
    - Snapshot reconciliation: match each payload segment to its persisted twin by `Id`; persisted active segments missing from the payload rejected (`TIMEENTRY_SEGMENT_NOT_FOUND` — Admin must use `DELETE /timeentry/segment/{id}` for removals); `ClockIn` change on existing segment rejected (`TIMEENTRY_SEGMENT_CLOCK_IN_LOCKED`); no-Id payload segments inserted with the supplied `ClockIn`/`ClockOut`.
    - Per-segment validation: `ClockIn` required (`TIMEENTRY_SEGMENT_CLOCK_IN_REQUIRED`); `ClockOut > ClockIn` when set (`TIMEENTRY_SEGMENT_CLOCK_OUT_BEFORE_CLOCK_IN`); day-bounds check as above; span ≤ 24h.
    - Cross-segment: no overlaps (`TIMEENTRY_SEGMENTS_OVERLAP`); at most one open segment (`TIMEENTRY_MULTIPLE_OPEN_SEGMENTS`); if an open segment exists it must be the last by `ClockIn ASC`.
  - Both branches: recompute parent totals via `Calculate(status, segments, Date, branchClock.LocalBusinessDateTime(branchClock.UtcNow()), settings...)`. Stamp parent `UpdatedAt`/`UpdatedByUserId` from one captured `branchClock.UtcNow()`. Commit once.
  Update `UpsertTimeEntryFluentValidation` to role-independent shape only: non-empty `OperatorId`, non-default `Date`, enum validity for `Status` and (when present) `Action`, plus per-segment shape checks gated by `.When(r => r.Segments != null)` (each segment has required `ClockIn`, optional `ClockOut > ClockIn` when set). **Do NOT enforce mutual exclusion of `Action` vs `Segments` in the validator** — that rule depends on the caller's role, and the validator runs before authentication so it has no role context. The four shape-gate combinations (Action null + Segments null, Action set + Segments set, Member with Segments, Admin with Action) are owned entirely by the use case in step "Dispatch by role × payload shape" above, where each combination returns its role-specific error key. Update `UpsertTimeEntryMapper.ToResponse` to project segments and recompute `IsInProgress`. Update `tests/CommonTestUtilities/Requests/RequestUpsertTimeEntryJsonBuilder.cs` with `BuildMemberOpenTap()` and `BuildMemberCloseTap()` (both set `Action`, leave `Segments` null), `BuildAdminSnapshot(params RequestTimeEntrySegmentJson[] segments)` (sets `Segments`, leaves `Action` null), and helpers like `WithAdminSegment(DateTime ClockIn, DateTime? ClockOut, Guid? Id = null)`. Rewrite `tests/UseCases.Test/UseCases/TimeEntries/Upsert/UpsertTimeEntryUseCaseTest.cs` covering:
  - Member `Action = Open` on empty state → opens a new segment (server-stamped ClockIn). `IsInProgress = true`.
  - Member `Action = Close` with open segment present → closes it (server-stamped ClockOut). `IsInProgress = false`.
  - Member `Action = Open` after all-closed → opens a new segment.
  - **Member retry `Action = Open` when open already exists** → idempotent no-op: same parent state returned, no new segment inserted (`Add` not received), `Commit` not received either since nothing changed, `parent.UpdatedAt` unchanged from prior call.
  - **Member retry `Action = Close` when no open exists** → idempotent no-op: same parent state returned, no mutation, `Commit` not received.
  - Member with `Segments != null` → `TIMEENTRY_MEMBER_SHOULD_NOT_SEND_SEGMENTS`.
  - Member with `Action == null` → `TIMEENTRY_MEMBER_TAP_ACTION_REQUIRED`.
  - Admin explicit-times snapshot with `ClockIn` (and optional `ClockOut`) and `Segments` populated → succeeds.
  - Admin with `Segments == null` → `TIMEENTRY_ADMIN_REQUIRES_SEGMENTS`.
  - Admin with `Action != null` → `TIMEENTRY_ADMIN_SHOULD_NOT_SEND_TAP_ACTION`.
  - Admin reconciliation: ClockIn-edit attempt → `TIMEENTRY_SEGMENT_CLOCK_IN_LOCKED`; missing-from-payload → `TIMEENTRY_SEGMENT_NOT_FOUND`.
  - Admin status change Present → Vacation with active segments → `TIMEENTRY_STATUS_CHANGE_REQUIRES_SEGMENT_CLEANUP`.
  - Overlap, out-of-day-bounds, multiple-open, ClockOut-before-ClockIn rejections (admin path).
  - Three-tap E2E (Member): `Action = Open` → `Action = Close` → `Action = Open` → `Action = Close`, asserting same `TimeEntry.Id` across all four taps, segment ids stable on close, `IsInProgress` flips, final totals computed via `Calculate`.
  - Four-tap retry E2E (Member): `Open` (success) → `Open` (dropped response, retry → no-op) → `Close` (success) → `Close` (dropped response, retry → no-op). Final state matches a clean two-tap sequence; no phantom segments or duplicate opens.
  - Overnight: Admin explicit-times snapshot with `{2026-05-08T22:00, 2026-05-09T06:00}` (single segment crossing midnight) succeeds.

#### Done criteria

- `TimeEntry` table has no `ClockIn`/`ClockOut` columns; `TimeEntrySegments` table exists with the filtered unique on open shifts.
- Member `PUT /timeentry` is a tap: payload carries `OperatorId`, `Date`, `Status`, and explicit `Action: "Open" | "Close"` with **no `Segments` field**. Server stamps the time. `Open` against an already-open parent is an idempotent no-op; `Close` against an already-closed parent is an idempotent no-op — retries always converge to the same state.
- Manager/Admin `PUT /timeentry` accepts an explicit `Segments` snapshot with DateTime `ClockIn` on every segment and **no `Action` field**; missing `Segments` returns 400 `TIMEENTRY_ADMIN_REQUIRES_SEGMENTS`; a non-null `Action` returns 400 `TIMEENTRY_ADMIN_SHOULD_NOT_SEND_TAP_ACTION`. Admin granular `POST/PUT/DELETE` on `/timeentry/segment` work end-to-end and recompute parent totals.
- Top-up lunch deduction verified by use-case tests across 10 min, 30 min, 1 h (exactly meets tier → no deduction), and 5 h gap cases; also verified for a live-running open segment with a preceding gap (the gap before an open segment is counted because the preceding segment is closed).
- Single-segment and multi-segment overnight shifts work via DateTime modeling — no rejection.
- Multi-open, overlap, out-of-day-bounds, status-change-with-segments, ClockOut-before-ClockIn, Member-with-segments, Member-without-Action, Admin-without-segments, and Admin-with-Action paths all return their documented error keys.
- `IX_TimeEntrySegments_TimeEntryId_OpenShift` race surfaces as 409 `TIMEENTRY_OPEN_SEGMENT_CONFLICT` via `PostgresExceptionHandler`.
- Spec sync at `Spec revision: v20` across all three documents.
  Note: Originally planned to land at v19 in Slice 3.6.A. Slice 3.6.B bumped to v20 to add a §6.7 clarification on Member overnight-Close routing (the `CanAllowPriorDayMemberClose` carve-out — prior-day open segments are closed by the next Member tap with no server-side reinterpretation across days).
- All three test suites pass; `bash server/docs/check-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes.

### Phase 4 — TimeEntry Read Path

Implement get/list with role-aware operator scope, pagination, and the Phase 3.5/3.6 read-time live-running recompute. The persisted `TotalHours` / `BalanceHours` for an in-progress current-day TimeEntry (any active segment with `ClockOut == null`) are a "last-write checkpoint" that may be stale; the read path must recompute them on every call so the API is the source of truth for live values.

**Read-time live-running recompute (used by both Get and List):** when a row has any active segment with `ClockOut == null`, the mapper replaces `TotalHours` / `BalanceHours` with `ITimeEntryCalculationService.Calculate(Status, Segments, Date, branchClock.LocalBusinessDateTime(branchClock.UtcNow()), DailyTargetHours, LunchDeductionOver6H, LunchDeductionOver4H)`. Rows where all segments are closed and non-Present statuses pass through unchanged. `IsInProgress` always mirrors "any active segment has `ClockOut == null`" and is computed by the mapper, not read from storage. Prior-day rows with a forgotten open segment surface their completed-segment hours plus `IsInProgress = true` (admin cleanup needed).

### Done criteria

- `TimeEntryStatus`, `TimeEntry`, `TimeEntrySegment`, and `Holiday` exist in Domain, Infrastructure, and the Milestone 5 migration/model snapshot
- `TimeEntry` enforces one active row per `(BranchId, OperatorId, Date)` through a filtered unique index; `TimeEntrySegment` enforces at most one open segment per parent through `IX_TimeEntrySegments_TimeEntryId_OpenShift` on `(TimeEntryId) WHERE ClockOut IS NULL AND Active = true`
- `Holiday` enforces one active row per `(BranchId, Date)` through a filtered unique index
- API-level PostgreSQL unique-violation translation returns clean `409` responses for TimeEntry, TimeEntrySegment open-shift, and Holiday uniqueness races
- `TimeEntry` carries `UpdatedAt` and `UpdatedByUserId`; `TimeEntrySegment` carries `UpdatedAt`/`UpdatedByUserId`; every TimeEntry upsert, admin segment endpoint, and deactivate stamps the parent (and where applicable, the segment) audit pair from one captured `branchClock.UtcNow()`
- `PUT /timeentry` is the only TimeEntry parent write/upsert path and uses a **dual-shape contract**: Member callers send `Action: Open | Close` with no `Segments` (server applies the action and stamps the clock; `Open` against an already-open parent and `Close` against an already-closed parent are both idempotent no-ops, so a dropped-response retry never duplicates or reverses state); Manager/Admin callers send an explicit `Segments` snapshot with DateTime `ClockIn` on every segment and no `Action`. Shape violations on either side return their documented role-shape error keys. The three admin granular endpoints (`POST /timeentry/{timeEntryId}/segment`, `PUT /timeentry/segment/{segmentId}`, `DELETE /timeentry/segment/{segmentId}`) handle explicit segment creation and surgical edits and recompute parent totals on every mutation.
- `TimeEntry` no longer holds `ClockIn`/`ClockOut` directly; clock pairs live on `TimeEntrySegment` rows as full `DateTime` values (branch-local wall clock, `timestamp without time zone`). Single-segment and multi-segment overnight shifts work via direct DateTime subtraction with no 24h wrap-around hack.
- `ITimeEntryCalculationService.Calculate(status, segments, entryDate, branchLocalNow, settings)` computes server-side `TotalHours` / `BalanceHours` with the **top-up gap-aware lunch rule**: `effective_lunch = max(0, lunch_tier − total_gap)` where `total_gap` counts every gap whose preceding segment is closed (including the gap before a trailing open segment).
- Response DTOs include `Segments: List<ResponseTimeEntrySegmentJson>` and the server-computed `IsInProgress` flag (`true` iff any active segment has `ClockOut == null`)
- TimeEntry read endpoints (`GET /timeentry/{id}` and `GET /timeentry`) recompute live-running values on every read using `IBranchClock.LocalBusinessDateTime` and the unified `Calculate` through a shared mapper helper; `Setting` and `branchLocalNow` are captured once per request and reused across all rows in a list page
- Members tap `PUT /timeentry` with `Action = Open` or `Action = Close` to open or close their own linked operator's same-day shift; the server stamps every clock value. Manager/Admin can upsert any branch operator's entry on any date with the explicit `Segments` snapshot. The three admin granular endpoints are Manager/Admin only and enforce `Setting.LockDate` per `LockDateGuard`.
- TimeEntry parent write (`PUT /timeentry`) is not blocked by `Setting.LockDate` — same as Phase 3.5. Admin granular segment write endpoints **do** enforce `LockDate` so surgical edits cannot retroactively reach into closed periods.
- TimeEntry read/list scope follows the agreed role rules: Members see only their linked operator; Manager/Admin see all branch entries; missing/cross-branch ids stay `404`
- Holiday CRUD is live: any branch role can read; only Manager/Admin can create/update/deactivate
- Holiday dates are immutable after creation; moving a holiday is handled by deactivate + create
- Holiday-aware due-date calculation is live for transaction create and auto-generated installment rows, skipping weekends and active branch holidays
- Manual cheque due dates remain explicit operator input and are not auto-adjusted by the holiday calendar
- Spec sync covers TimeEntry / TimeEntrySegment audit fields, Holiday constraints, the unified Calculate signature with top-up lunch and DateTime overnight semantics, the dual-shape PUT contract, the admin granular segment endpoints, Holiday contract, and holiday-aware due-date behavior; shared revision metadata is at `v20` and `check-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes
- Validator, use-case, Web API, and architecture tests pass with the new TimeEntry, TimeEntrySegment, Holiday, and due-date behavior

## Milestone 6 — Configuration & Lookup Admin CRUD

**Goal:** Add admin maintenance for the seeded lookup and config entities — `Category`, `TransactionType`, `Product`, `Setting` — bootstrapped by `CreateBranch` in Milestone 1 but never reachable from an admin surface. Add a Brazilian federal-holiday source so branch admins can bulk-import the standard national calendar into the per-branch `Holiday` table that M5 ships, instead of typing 10 to 13 dates by hand.

**Scope boundary:** Branch-scoped CRUD for `Category`, `TransactionType`, `Product`, and `Setting` restricted to `Admin` and `Manager`. Read endpoints on those entities are available to any authenticated branch role so other modules (e.g. transaction creation) can resolve names. The Brazilian holiday source is a pure-function service plus a thin `POST /holiday/import-br/{year}?includeOptionalFederal=true|false` orchestrator over the existing `IHolidaysRepository`; no new domain entity, no schema change in the holiday model, no external network dependency in M6. No reporting endpoints — those land in Milestone 7. The system-only `Diferença Caixa` product is protected against rename and deactivate through admin CRUD; `DisplayOrder` adjustment on that row IS allowed (admins legitimately need to control the daily-close form layout).

**Precondition:** Milestones 1–5 are fully closed. The Holiday CRUD slice (M5 Phase 2), the `IBranchHolidaySource` adapter (M5 Phase 1), the `LockDateGuard` (Application/Services/Settings), the `MemberAccountScopeResolver`, the `ICashVarianceProductResolver` (M4 Phase 4) for the Diferença Caixa guard, and the `PostgresExceptionHandler` unique-constraint translation pattern are already available. The seeded entities themselves already exist in Domain and the M1 migration — this milestone adds the admin surface on top, plus the migration that converts their UNIQUE indexes to filtered-on-`Active` shape (M2/M5 convention).

**Key behaviors:**

- `Category`, `TransactionType`, and `Product` uniqueness becomes filtered on `Active = true` so soft-deactivated rows do not block re-creating an active row with the same name later. Pre-M6 these three indexes are plain UNIQUE — once admin deactivate ships, a deactivated row would permanently lock up its name with no API recovery (a real production trap). The Phase 1 migration converts the indexes in a single pass and updates `PostgresExceptionHandler` mappings accordingly. There is no "reactivate" endpoint — re-create after deactivate is a fresh row with a new `Id`, preserving the old row for audit.
- `Category`: each slice ships five use cases — Create, List, Get, Update, Deactivate. Name unique per active branch row. `DefaultDirection` is fixed at creation (cannot change after — historical transactions tied to this category already inherited the direction at create time per §6.1 classification invariant). Deactivate is soft and cascades to the category's `TransactionType` children (one-way; recreating the category later does not auto-reactivate children — same convention as M2 Account → OperatorAccount).
- `TransactionType`: same five use cases. `Name`, `SettlementRule`, and `RequiresTabAccountAndClient` are all editable post-create; changes are forward-only (existing `Transaction` rows already snapshotted `Direction`, `CategoryId`, and `DueDate` at create time per §6.1, so the edit never retro-changes ledger data). `(CategoryId, Name)` filtered unique is enforced. Cross-category move (`CategoryId` change) is **not** supported in M6 — moving a TransactionType between categories is rare and would require careful Direction-invariant analysis; treated as "deactivate the old + create a new one under the target category" for now.
- `Product`: same five use cases. Name unique per active branch row. `DisplayOrder` management. The system-only `Diferença Caixa` product (resolved by name via `ICashVarianceProductResolver` since M4) is protected: admin Update may not rename it (400 `PRODUCT_SYSTEM_PROTECTED`) and admin Deactivate must refuse it (409 `PRODUCT_SYSTEM_PROTECTED`); `DisplayOrder` mutation on the system product IS allowed. Re-creating a deactivated product (fresh `Id`, same name) is allowed once the filtered-unique migration lands.
- `Setting`: update only (one row per branch, created by `CreateBranch`; no create or deactivate endpoint). `LockDate`, `DailyTargetHours`, `LunchDeductionOver6H`, `LunchDeductionOver4H` are all editable. `LockDate` is a one-way ratchet — admin may only advance it forward in time (rejecting a request that would move it backwards prevents accidentally unlocking past financial periods). The three TimeEntry-related fields take effect on the next calc service invocation (read endpoints recompute on every read per M5 Phase 4, so even in-progress entries pick up the new tier values on the next read).
- **Brazilian holiday calendar source**: a pure-function `IBrazilianHolidayCalendar` service returns the canonical Brazilian calendar for a given year, classified into two groups:
  - **Mandatory national holidays (`feriado nacional`)**, by federal law: 10 entries — `1/1` Confraternização Universal, `Sexta-feira Santa` (Easter − 2 days, by Law 9.093/1995), `21/4` Tiradentes, `1/5` Dia do Trabalho, `7/9` Independência, `12/10` Nossa Senhora Aparecida, `2/11` Finados, `15/11` Proclamação da República, `20/11` Consciência Negra (federal since Law 14.759/2023), `25/12` Natal.
  - **Curated operational subset of MGI optional federal "pontos facultativos"**: 3 entries — `Carnaval (terça)` = Easter − 47 days, `Quarta-feira de Cinzas (até 14h)` = Easter − 46 days, `Corpus Christi` = Easter + 60 days. These three are the most consistently observed by Brazilian businesses (loterias, retail, banking) and are deterministically computable from Easter, which makes them safe to ship as a bulk-import default. **MGI's full annual optional-federal calendar is broader** — it can additionally include Carnival Monday, the Corpus Christi bridge Friday, Christmas Eve and New Year's Eve afternoons, Dia do Servidor Público (28/10), and other bridge days that vary year-to-year and require an annual MGI decree. Those are intentionally **not** included in the M6 import default because they are administrative/observance choices (not fixed by federal law and not Easter-derivable), and bulk-importing them risks creating holidays many branches would not actually observe. Admins who want any additional MGI optional day for a specific year add it manually via the existing per-branch `POST /holiday` endpoint from M5 Phase 2 — that path already supports custom descriptions and arbitrary dates. These three are *not* mandatory by federal law; state laws may make them mandatory locally.
  - The endpoint contract: `GET /holiday/import-br/{year}/preview?includeOptionalFederal=true|false` (any branch role) and `POST /holiday/import-br/{year}?includeOptionalFederal=true|false` (Manager/Admin). When the query param is omitted or `false`, only the 10 mandatory holidays are returned/imported. When `true`, the 3 curated optional federal entries are appended in date order. Preview shows `AlreadyExists = true` for any date the branch already has an active `Holiday` for; import skips those instead of erroring (idempotent — re-running the same call returns the same set with everything marked `Skipped`).
  - **Reliability design (deferred multi-source):** the M6 implementation is a single pure-function source (computed from fixed dates + the Anonymous Gregorian Easter algorithm). Reliability comes from (a) the algorithm being deterministic with no network or vendor dependency, and (b) snapshot tests pinning the output for years 2025-2030 against externally verified date lists. The service is intentionally exposed via an `IBrazilianHolidayCalendar` abstraction so a future milestone can add secondary/fallback sources (e.g., brasilapi.com.br for new-law detection or the full annual MGI optional list) behind a composite implementation without touching the import use case. Adding live API integration is explicitly out of scope for M6; the per-branch manual `POST /holiday` from M5 remains the immediate override for any year where the computed calendar needs an unscheduled adjustment or an MGI optional outside the curated subset.
- All admin write endpoints are Manager/Admin only. Read endpoints on `Category`, `TransactionType`, and `Product` are open to any authenticated branch role (other features list/resolve them by id). `Setting` read is open to any authenticated branch role.
- Spec sync: §3.9, §3.10, §3.13 line their `Unique constraint:` lines to reflect the filtered-on-`Active` shape (Phase 1 makes this a factual spec change, not just a revision note). §3.18 `Setting` is unchanged. The Brazilian federal-holiday list (both mandatory and optional federal groups) is documented as a new §5.1 appendix in `loto-specs.md` so the curated set is reviewable in code review, not buried in C# constants alone.

---

### Phase 1 — Filtered-Unique Migration for Lookup Tables

Convert `Category`, `TransactionType`, and `Product` UNIQUE indexes to filtered-on-`Active` shape so admin deactivate doesn't permanently lock up the name. This is a tiny migration with a measurable behavior change; it must ship before any admin Create/Deactivate endpoint goes live, so test suites and integration runs operate against the corrected schema from the start of the milestone.

- [x] **1.1** Update `server.Infrastructure/Configurations/CategoryConfiguration.cs`: change `HasIndex(c => new { c.BranchId, c.Name }).IsUnique()` to add `.HasDatabaseName("IX_Categories_BranchId_Name").HasFilter("\"Active\" = true")`. Use the explicit database name so `PostgresExceptionHandler` can key it off.
- [x] **1.2** Update `server.Infrastructure/Configurations/TransactionTypeConfiguration.cs`: change `HasIndex(t => new { t.CategoryId, t.Name }).IsUnique()` to add `.HasDatabaseName("IX_TransactionTypes_CategoryId_Name").HasFilter("\"Active\" = true")`.
- [x] **1.3** Update `server.Infrastructure/Configurations/ProductConfiguration.cs`: change `HasIndex(p => new { p.BranchId, p.Name }).IsUnique()` to add `.HasDatabaseName("IX_Products_BranchId_Name").HasFilter("\"Active\" = true")`.
- [x] **1.4** Generate EF migration `LookupFilteredActiveUniqueIndexes` covering the three index changes. The migration must drop and re-create each index (PostgreSQL cannot add a filter to an existing UNIQUE index in place). Verify the generated `Up` and `Down` are clean (no extra column changes).
- [x] **1.5** Extend `server.API/ExceptionHandling/PostgresExceptionHandler.UniqueConstraintMessages` with three new entries: `IX_Categories_BranchId_Name → CATEGORY_NAME_CONFLICT`, `IX_TransactionTypes_CategoryId_Name → TRANSACTION_TYPE_NAME_CONFLICT`, `IX_Products_BranchId_Name → PRODUCT_NAME_CONFLICT`. Add the three resource keys to `ResourcesErrorMessages.resx` and `.Designer.cs` with Portuguese text mirroring the `HOLIDAY_DATE_CONFLICT` / `CLIENT_CPF_CONFLICT` style. Naming follows the existing `TRANSACTION_TYPE_*` convention from M3 (underscore between `TRANSACTION` and `TYPE`).
- [x] **1.6** Extend `PostgresExceptionHandlerTest` `KnownUniqueConstraintMessages` with the three new mappings.
- [x] **1.7** Add three `tests/WebApi.Test/{Category,TransactionType,Product}/{Entity}NameUniquenessConstraintTest.cs` files mirroring `ClientCpfUniquenessConstraintTest` from Milestone 2: each test seeds two active rows with the same name and asserts the second insert throws `DbUpdateException` with `SqlState = "23505"` and the new constraint name. Also include a "same name with one row inactive should succeed" case to prove the filter is correctly applied.

Single-row-per-branch update. No Create (bootstrapped by `CreateBranch`). No Deactivate. Read is open to any branch role; writes are Manager/Admin. `LockDate` is a one-way ratchet — admin may only advance it.

### Phase 6 — Brazilian Holiday Calendar Source

Add an opt-in bulk-import path so a branch admin can populate the per-branch `Holiday` table with the standard Brazilian calendar for a given year in one request, instead of typing dates by hand. The calendar is classified into two groups (mandatory national holidays vs optional federal "pontos facultativos") so admins can choose what to import. Pure-function service + thin orchestrator over the existing M5 Holiday batch path. No external network dependency in M6 — see the source-reliability note in the milestone key behaviors.

- [x] **6.1** Add a public, branch-agnostic service `IBrazilianHolidayCalendar` under `server.Application/Services/Holidays/` with the method `IReadOnlyList<BrazilianHolidayEntry> GetForYear(int year, bool includeOptionalFederal)` returning entries in ascending date order. The record is `public sealed record BrazilianHolidayEntry(DateOnly Date, string Description, BrazilianHolidayType Type)` and the enum is `public enum BrazilianHolidayType { National = 0, OptionalFederal = 1 }`. Pure function — no I/O, no DateTime.Now reads, no DI dependencies. Default implementation `BrazilianHolidayCalendar` is registered as a singleton in `AppDependencyInjection`. The interface is intentionally minimal so a future milestone can add a composite (multi-source / fallback) implementation behind the same contract without touching the use case.
- [x] **6.2** Implement the calendar with this exact list per the spec appendix (item 6.12 below — `loto-specs.md` §5.1).
  - **National (`Type = National`, 10 entries — mandatory by federal law):** `1/1` Confraternização Universal, `Sexta-feira Santa` (Easter − 2 days, by Law 9.093/1995), `21/4` Tiradentes, `1/5` Dia do Trabalho, `7/9` Independência, `12/10` Nossa Senhora Aparecida, `2/11` Finados, `15/11` Proclamação da República, `20/11` Consciência Negra (by Law 14.759/2023), `25/12` Natal.
  - **OptionalFederal (`Type = OptionalFederal`, 3 entries — curated operational subset of the MGI annual optional calendar):** `Carnaval (terça)` = Easter − 47 days, `Quarta-feira de Cinzas (até 14h)` = Easter − 46 days, `Corpus Christi` = Easter + 60 days. This is a deliberate shortlist of the three optional dates most consistently observed by Brazilian businesses and the three that are deterministically Easter-anchored. The full MGI annual optional calendar is broader (Carnival Monday, Corpus Christi bridge Friday, eve-day afternoons, Dia do Servidor Público, etc.) and varies year-to-year by federal decree — those are intentionally NOT in the M6 default and remain available through the existing per-branch `POST /holiday` endpoint.
  - Compute Easter Sunday via the Anonymous Gregorian algorithm (Meeus/Jones/Butcher form — standard and dependency-free). When `includeOptionalFederal` is `false`, return only the National 10 in ascending date order. When `true`, return all 13 in ascending date order. Validate year is `>= 1900 && <= 2200` to keep the algorithm in tested territory; outside the range throw `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` (system-level — the controller catches the route-range case earlier so this should never reach the user-facing surface in normal flow).
- [x] **6.3** Add `UseCases.Test/Services/Holidays/BrazilianHolidayCalendarTest.cs` covering: known-year snapshots for 2025, 2026, 2027, 2028, 2029, 2030 (six explicit year tests, each asserting the exact dates and descriptions returned with `includeOptionalFederal = true`, cross-checked against an externally-verified source); count is exactly 10 when `includeOptionalFederal = false`, exactly 13 when `true`; ascending date order in both modes; year out of range throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`; `National`-typed entries are a strict subset of the full set; `OptionalFederal`-typed entries are the three Easter-anchored optionals only.
- [x] **6.4** Add Communication DTOs: `ResponseBrazilianHolidayPreviewJson { int Year, bool IncludesOptionalFederal, IReadOnlyList<ResponseBrazilianHolidayPreviewItemJson> Items }` and `ResponseBrazilianHolidayPreviewItemJson { DateOnly Date, string Description, BrazilianHolidayType Type, bool AlreadyExists }`. For the import response: `ResponseBrazilianHolidayImportJson { int Year, bool IncludesOptionalFederal, IReadOnlyList<ResponseBrazilianHolidayImportItemJson> Items, int ImportedCount, int SkippedCount }` and `ResponseBrazilianHolidayImportItemJson { DateOnly Date, string Description, BrazilianHolidayType Type, BrazilianHolidayImportStatus Status }` where the enum is `Imported = 0, Skipped = 1`. The `BrazilianHolidayType` enum and the `Status` enum both live under `server.Domain.Entities.Enums` per the M5 convention (or under `server.Communication.Enums` if the project established that pattern elsewhere — check the existing codebase).
- [x] **6.5** Add the M6 resource keys needed by the import slice: `HOLIDAY_IMPORT_YEAR_OUT_OF_RANGE` ("O ano informado deve estar entre 1900 e 2200") — used by the controller's route-range validation.
- [x] **6.6** Implement `PreviewBrazilianHolidayImportUseCase` under `UseCases/Holidays/PreviewBrazilianImport/`: authenticate → any branch role (preview is a read) → load existing active branch holidays for the year via a new `IHolidaysRepository.ListActiveDatesByBranchIdAndYearAsNoTracking(branchId, year)` (the typical branch has < 50 holidays so an in-memory filter off the existing `ListActiveDatesByBranchIdAsNoTracking` is also acceptable; the dedicated method is cheap to add and explicit about intent) → for each entry from `IBrazilianHolidayCalendar.GetForYear(year, includeOptionalFederal)`, set `AlreadyExists = branchActiveDates.Contains(date)` → return.
- [x] **6.7** Implement `ImportBrazilianHolidaysUseCase` under `UseCases/Holidays/ImportBrazilian/`: authenticate → role check (Manager/Admin) → load existing branch active dates for the year → for each calendar entry (filtered by `includeOptionalFederal`): if already exists → `Skipped`; otherwise instantiate a `Holiday` (carrying the entry's `Description`) and call `IHolidaysRepository.Add(holiday)` → `Imported`. Single `Commit` at the end. The use case relies on the existing `(BranchId, Date) WHERE Active = true` filtered unique as a backstop against concurrent inserts (translated to `HOLIDAY_DATE_CONFLICT` by `PostgresExceptionHandler`); the pre-check avoids the conflict in the normal case so the import returns a clean per-item summary. The import is idempotent — running it twice on the same year + flag returns the same set with every item marked `Skipped` on the second call.

  - `POST /holiday/import-br/{year:int:min(1900):max(2200)}?includeOptionalFederal=true|false` (Manager/Admin) returning `ResponseBrazilianHolidayImportJson`.

Update `loto_presentation.html` and `loto_entity_relationship_diagram.html` only if a sync-group factual change requires it (the new endpoint is application-level, not entity-level — the ERD does not need an update). ### Phase 6.5 — External Brazilian Holiday Providers (composite + provenance)

Bring the M6 Phase 6 "deferred multi-source" hint into the present. Add two no-key external providers (BrasilAPI and Nager.Date) behind a composite resolver, dedupe via a concept catalog so providers and canonical Easter math stay aligned even when descriptions differ, and tag each persisted `Holiday` row with its `Source` so a future audit can answer "where did this row come from?". The annual cron is out of scope — Phase 6.5 ships **routes only**; an external scheduler (cron, Cloud Scheduler, a manual click) drives the import once a year, before Jan 1 of the following year, so day-one of the new year already has its calendar.

**Scope boundary:** Multi-source preview/import for the **national + curated optional federal** calendar already shipped in Phase 6 (10/13 entries). No state holidays, no per-branch state code, no description-override path, no scheduled background service, no schema change to `Branch`. The only new schema column is `Holiday.Source`. State-level holidays remain available exclusively via the manual `POST /holiday` endpoint from M5 Phase 2.

**Key behaviors:**

- **Composite is the new default.** `source=Composite` walks Nager → BrasilAPI → Canonical in priority order. For each of the 13 canonical concepts, the highest-priority provider that returns a name-matching row claims it (with that provider's date and description); concepts unclaimed after all providers run are backfilled from Canonical. Explicit `source=Canonical|BrasilApi|Nager` calls a single source. The 502 `HOLIDAY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE` path is unreachable in Composite mode because Canonical never fails — it only fires when a single external source is requested explicitly and that source times out or returns invalid data.
- **Concept catalog drives identity.** The 13 canonical Brazilian holidays each map to a stable `ConceptId` (e.g. `TIRADENTES`, `CARNAVAL_TERCA`, `SEXTA_FEIRA_SANTA`) with an `ExpectedDateForYear` function (reusing the existing Meeus/Jones/Butcher Easter algorithm) and accent-stripped name-match patterns. Provider rows are matched to concepts by **name pattern first, then date proximity (±3 days)** when multiple candidates share a name (Nager returns two "Carnaval" rows for Mon+Tue — the one nearer Easter−47 wins). Provider rows that match no concept are dropped silently. Provider rows that match by name but are more than ±3 days from the expected date still match (the winning source's date overrides expected) — this is deliberate so a future provider update or a leap-year corner case doesn't silently drop a real holiday.
- **`Holiday.Source` provenance.** New enum `HolidaySource { Manual = 0, Canonical = 1, BrasilApi = 2, Nager = 3 }` on the `Holiday` entity, default `Manual`. M5 `POST /holiday` sets `Manual` on inserted rows; the M6 Phase 6 canonical import sets `Canonical`; the new composite/external paths set per-row Source per the catalog resolution. The column is informational — no business rule reads it. The migration backfills every existing row to `Manual` (correct for any row predating Phase 6 imports and indistinguishable from a hand-entry).
- **Idempotency and skip-existing are preserved unchanged.** A second composite run for the same year on the same branch returns every item as `Skipped` and writes nothing. The existing `(BranchId, Date) WHERE Active = true` filtered unique index is the race backstop. Inactive (soft-deleted) rows on the same date continue to be treated as "not existing" — a fresh active row is inserted alongside the inactive one, matching M6 Phase 6 behavior.
- **Per-row `Source` field in responses.** `ResponseBrazilianHolidayPreviewItemJson` and `ResponseBrazilianHolidayImportItemJson` gain a `Source` tag so the caller can audit which provider contributed each entry (e.g. for Quarta-feira de Cinzas which is always `Canonical` because neither provider returns it). The top-level `Source` query param the caller requested is echoed back in `ResponseBrazilianHolidayPreviewJson` and `ResponseBrazilianHolidayImportJson` for round-trip clarity.
- **External HTTP I/O lives in Infrastructure, normalization in Application.** Provider HTTP clients are typed `IHttpClientFactory` wrappers in `server.Infrastructure/Holidays/External/` returning provider-specific DTOs. Interfaces (`IBrasilApiHolidayProvider`, `INagerDateHolidayProvider`) live in `server.Domain/Interfaces/` per the JWT services precedent. The concept catalog, the composite resolver, and the per-source normalizer live in `server.Application/Services/Holidays/`. "Provider" suffix avoids clashing with the existing `IBranchHolidaySource` (M5).
- **Infrastructure mustn’t throw backend exceptions.** Per `server/CLAUDE.md`, Infrastructure depends on `server.Domain` only and never references `server.Exceptions`. The HTTP provider clients therefore communicate failure through a domain result object (`BrazilianHolidayProviderResult<TDto>` in `server.Domain.Models/`), mirroring the `TokenResultValidation` pattern that `JwtTokenService` already uses to signal recoverable failure to `AuthenticationService`. The Application-layer resolver receives the result and is the only place that translates a failed result into `ExternalProviderUnavailableException(HOLIDAY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE)`.
- **Year coverage is bounded by operational convention, not by code.** The route-range constraint stays `min(1900):max(2200)` from M6 Phase 6 — callers can still manually preview/import any year in that range. The recommended **cron operational pattern** is a single annual run in late December that imports `currentYear + 1`, so day-one of the new year already has its calendar populated; an optional `+ 2` overshoot is documented as safe and idempotent because re-running the same year is all-Skipped. The cron itself is external to this repo and Phase 6.5 does not ship scheduling code; this expectation is documented in `loto-specs.md` §5.1 (item 6.5.21) so operators know what cadence to wire up.
- **Defaults stay safe.** `includeOptionalFederal=false` unchanged. `source=Composite` is the new default; existing M6 Phase 6 integration tests that pass no `source` param continue to pass — composite + the 10 nationals + 0 existing rows produces the same 10 inserts they previously asserted on, just with `Source = Nager | BrasilApi | Canonical` per concept instead of all-Canonical. Tests assert by **count and date set**, not by `Source`, so they don't break.
- **Spec sync** updates §3.17 (`Holiday` gains the `Source` column), expands §5.1 with the composite ordering + concept catalog + provider quirks (BrasilAPI's "Confraternização mundial" rename, Nager's regional rows being dropped, both providers missing Quarta-feira de Cinzas → always Canonical backfill). The ERD's `Holiday` entity card adds the `Source` column. `loto_presentation.html` mirrors the §5.1 update. Bump shared `Spec revision` to v22 across the sync group.

The resolver is the **single owner of the Infrastructure-failure → backend-exception translation**: any `BrazilianHolidayProviderResult` with `Success = false` is converted into `ExternalProviderUnavailableException(ResourcesErrorMessages.HOLIDAY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE)` here, never inside the Infrastructure clients.
  - `source = Canonical`: delegate to existing `IBrazilianHolidayCalendar`, tag every entry `Source = Canonical`. Synchronous wrapped in `Task.FromResult`.
  - `source = BrasilApi` or `Nager`: call the corresponding provider; if the returned `BrazilianHolidayProviderResult.Success` is `false`, throw `ExternalProviderUnavailableException`. On success, map provider DTOs into concept-tagged entries via the catalog's name+date matching (±3 days tolerance), drop unmatched provider rows, backfill missing concepts from `IBrazilianHolidayCalendar` with `Source = Canonical`, return.
  - `source = Composite`: walk providers in order `[Nager, BrasilApi]`, treating each provider's failed `BrazilianHolidayProviderResult` as "skip this provider, log the FailureReason, continue to the next" — Composite **never throws** `ExternalProviderUnavailableException` because Canonical backfills any concepts left unclaimed after both providers fail. Accumulate concept claims by priority; any concept still unclaimed after all providers, backfill from `IBrazilianHolidayCalendar` with `Source = Canonical`.
  - All four paths respect `includeOptionalFederal`: when `false`, return only the 10 National-typed concepts.

Added `[ProducesResponseType(... Status403Forbidden)]` on the six read endpoints. >   - **Brazilian import 400 + 409 are reachable; declarations kept.** The 400 path on both `GET /holiday/import-br/{year}/preview` and `POST /holiday/import-br/{year}` is reachable via ASP.NET model-binding rejection of invalid `?source` enum values (and similarly for `?includeOptionalFederal`), even though the route's `min(1900):max(2200)` constraint guards the year. The 409 path on `POST /holiday/import-br/{year}` is reachable via a concurrent-insert race against the `IX_Holidays_BranchId_Date` filtered unique index: between the use case's `ListActiveDatesByBranchIdAndYearAsNoTracking` pre-check and the `UnitOfWork.Commit`, another writer can land an active row on the same date — the commit then hits `SqlState 23505`, and `PostgresExceptionHandler.Normalize` translates it to `ConflictException` → 409. Both declarations are correct and now have backing tests.
>   - **Filled gaps for declared-but-untested codes.** Added the following new tests so every declared code on the five M6 controllers is hit by at least one WebApi.Test method:

>     - `HolidayControllerImportBrazilianUnhappyPathTest`: `PreviewBrazilianImport × 400` and `ImportBrazilian × 400` (invalid-enum `?source` value triggers model-binding 400 before the action runs). `ImportBrazilian × 409` simulates the filtered-unique race window by registering a `RaceWindowHolidaysRepository` decorator over the real `server.Infrastructure.Repositories.HolidaysRepository` (via `WithWebHostBuilder` + `RemoveAll`/`AddScoped`): the decorator returns an empty existing-dates list while the database actually carries an active Jan 1 row, so the use case stages an insert, `UnitOfWork.Commit` hits 23505, and the response surfaces 409 + `HOLIDAY_DATE_CONFLICT`.

### Done criteria

- The `Category`, `TransactionType`, and `Product` UNIQUE indexes are filtered on `Active = true`; the corresponding `PostgresExceptionHandler` mappings translate the filtered-unique violations to `409 *_NAME_CONFLICT` keys; constraint-level tests prove the migration applied and the filter behaves as expected.
- `Category` CRUD is live with Create / List / Get / Update / Deactivate. `DefaultDirection` is fixed at creation. Deactivate cascades soft-deactivation to child `TransactionType` rows; cascade is one-way (re-creating a category later does not auto-restore children).
- `TransactionType` CRUD is live with Create / List / Get / Update / Deactivate. `CategoryId` is immutable post-create (no cross-category move in M6). Updates to `Name`, `SettlementRule`, and `RequiresTabAccountAndClient` are forward-only — historical `Transaction` rows are unaffected because they snapshotted `Direction`, `CategoryId`, and `DueDate` at create time per §6.1.
- `Product` CRUD is live with Create / List / Get / Update / Deactivate. The system-only `Diferença Caixa` product is protected from rename and deactivate via `ICashVarianceProductResolver`; admins may still adjust its `DisplayOrder`. Re-creating a deactivated product (fresh `Id`, same name) is supported by the Phase 1 filtered-unique index — there is no explicit "reactivate" endpoint.
- `Setting` Get and Update are live; no Create, no Deactivate (single bootstrapped row per branch). `LockDate` is a one-way ratchet — backwards moves are rejected with `SETTING_LOCK_DATE_RETREAT`. `DailyTargetHours` / `LunchDeductionOver6H` / `LunchDeductionOver4H` updates take effect on the next M5 read-path recompute without any explicit cache invalidation.
- `IBrazilianHolidayCalendar` (Phase 6) computes the canonical Brazilian calendar for a given year — 10 mandatory national holidays (by federal law) plus, when `includeOptionalFederal = true`, a curated 3-entry operational subset of the MGI annual optional federal calendar (Carnaval terça, Quarta-feira de Cinzas até 14h, Corpus Christi — the three most consistently observed by businesses; other MGI optionals like Carnival Monday, the Corpus Christi bridge Friday, or the eve-day afternoons remain available via the existing per-branch `POST /holiday`). This canonical source stays addressable via explicit `source=Canonical`; after Phase 6.5 the same data is also derived from `BrazilianHolidayConceptCatalog`, so the two paths produce byte-identical output.
- **Brazilian holiday import endpoints are live with multi-source composite + provenance (Phase 6 baseline + Phase 6.5 composite).** Both `GET /holiday/import-br/{year}/preview?includeOptionalFederal=true|false&source=Composite|Canonical|BrasilApi|Nager` (any branch role) and `POST /holiday/import-br/{year}?includeOptionalFederal=true|false&source=...` (Manager/Admin) share the same source-dispatch contract. `source` defaults to `Composite` (Phase 6.5); when omitted, the route behaves exactly as a Composite call — existing M6 Phase 6 callers and tests continue to pass because Composite + zero existing rows still produces the same 10/13 inserts, just with mixed per-row `Source` tags instead of all-Canonical. Composite walks Nager → BrasilAPI → Canonical in priority order, deduping via the 13 canonical concepts in `BrazilianHolidayConceptCatalog` (name-match + ±3-day date proximity tiebreaker), and backfills unclaimed concepts from Canonical. Preview returns the resolved list with `AlreadyExists` flags so the admin UI can confirm; Import inserts only missing dates into the branch's `Holiday` table, returning per-item `Imported` / `Skipped` status. The import is idempotent — a second call on the same year+flag is a no-op (all-Skipped) regardless of source. Single-source `Nager` / `BrasilApi` calls return 502 `HOLIDAY_SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE` on provider timeout / invalid response; Composite swallows individual provider failures because Canonical backfills any leftover concepts. Every persisted `Holiday` row carries a `Source` enum (`Manual=0, Canonical=1, BrasilApi=2, Nager=3`); existing rows backfill to `Manual` via the Phase 6.5 migration. M5 `POST /holiday` writes `Source = Manual`; the canonical-only import path writes `Source = Canonical`; the multi-source path writes per-row `Source` per the resolver's per-entry tag. Both preview and import responses include the per-row `Source` and the requested top-level `Source` echo so the UI can audit provenance.
- State-level holidays remain explicitly out of scope: branch admins add regional / municipal / branch-specific dates via the existing M5 `POST /holiday` endpoint (which sets `Source = Manual`). A future milestone may add a `Branch.StateCode` column and state-aware provider dispatch; Phase 6.5 intentionally defers that.
- Read endpoints on `Category`, `TransactionType`, `Product`, `Setting`, and `Holiday` (including the two import-br actions) are open to any authenticated branch role; write endpoints are Manager/Admin only. Architecture tests confirm explicit auth intent across all new endpoints.
- Validator, use-case, Web API, and architecture tests pass with the new admin CRUD surfaces, the Brazilian holiday canonical import, and the Phase 6.5 multi-source composite + provenance additions. `check-loto-doc-sync.sh` passes at `Spec revision: v22` (Phase 6 added §5.1 at v21; Phase 6.5 added the Sources subsection and §3.17 `Source` column at v22).

## Milestone 7 — Reporting & Reconciliation

**Goal:** Add manager reporting dashboards (daily ledger, cash-variance summary, monthly reconciliation), fiado aging views (per-client balance, per-row aging, per-origin open-cheque aging), operator and time-entry period summaries, and three `POST` "compute without persist" preview endpoints (installment plan preview, transaction edit impact preview, create transaction impact preview) — all on top of the read surfaces Milestones 3–6 already shipped.

**Scope boundary:** Read-only query endpoints and computed projections. The three preview endpoints are non-persisting and each reuses **its write twin's request DTO verbatim** (installment → `RequestCreateTransactionInstallmentJson`, edit → `RequestUpdateTransactionJson`, create → `RequestCreateTransactionJson`) plus the write twin's live validation, branch-consistency, scope, and lock-date paths. No new write flows. No new lifecycle states. No schema changes. Lock-date advancement continues to flow through `PUT /setting` from Milestone 6 Phase 5; this milestone gives managers the views they consult before pushing the date.

**Precondition:** Milestones 3–6 are fully closed and producing data. The transaction read scope (§6.10), the transaction mutation contract (§6.11), `MemberAccountScopeResolver` / `MemberAccountScopeGuard`, `LockDateGuard`, `IBranchClock`, `ICashVarianceProductResolver`, `ICashVarianceCalculator`, `DueDateCalculator`, `InstallmentPlanBuilder`, `ITimeEntryCalculationService`, `IBranchHolidaySource`, `TransactionBranchConsistencyService`, `TransactionMutationPermissionGuard`, and `PostgresExceptionHandler` are already available.

**Explicit deferrals (called out in the original sketch):** Bounced-cheque lifecycle (`TransactionStatus.Bounced` / `BouncedAt`) and operator-entered-vs-auto-generated installment provenance are intentionally not part of M7. Both would be write flows against the ledger and would violate the read-only scope boundary. Noted at the end of this milestone as candidate M7.5 phases if operational signal emerges.

**Key behaviors:**

- All M7 endpoints are read-only or compute-only. None call `Add`, none mutate persisted state, none open a unit of work.
- **Preview/write parity** for the three preview endpoints is pinned by behavior tests (preview throws the same exception write does on the same invalid input) plus end-to-end determinism tests (preview output matches what the real write produces / would predict). No interface extraction, no `Received()` assertions on validators or shared collaborators — the codebase's convention is to wire dependencies through test builders, so behavior tests are the honest gate.
- **Permission model.** Three buckets:
  - **Manager/Admin only** (whole-branch financial views): daily ledger, fiado balance, fiado aging, open-cheque aging, cash variance summary, monthly reconciliation, transaction edit impact preview.
  - **Any branch role with scope** (operator-self): operator transaction summary, time-entry balance summary. Members see their linked operator only; Manager/Admin see any branch operator. Member with no linked operator → empty summary (list-style short-circuit), or 403 with the existing `TIMEENTRY_REQUIRES_OPERATOR_LINK` / `TRANSACTION_MEMBER_REQUIRES_OPERATOR_LINK` for targeted reads. Member targeting another operator → 403 `REPORT_MEMBER_NOT_OWN_OPERATOR`.
  - **Same as the write twin** (installment preview and create impact preview): Member with linked operator + account scope, Manager/Admin elevated — each preview runs through the same `TransactionCreatePreamble` (linked-operator + account-scope enforcement) as its write twin, so anyone who can create the row can preview that same create, and every impact section stays scoped to the previewed `(account, client, date)`. **Edit impact preview is the deliberate exception**: Manager/Admin even though its write twin `PUT /transaction/{id}` allows Members within scope, because it previews an edit to an existing persisted row loaded by id (not a scope-limited create) and is positioned as a manager review tool.
- **Aging buckets** are server-side per §6.14: `Current` (future-due), `Days0To30`, `Days31To60`, `Days61To90`, `Days91Plus`. Boundary semantics: day 30 → `Days0To30`; day 31 → `Days31To60`; day 90 → `Days61To90`; day 91+ → `Days91Plus`. The bucketizer is a static pure function (DueDateCalculator precedent), not an interface or DI service.
- **Excluded transaction statuses.** All financial reports filter on `Status = Active` AND `Active = true` (entity-base soft-delete). `Draft` and `Cancelled` are excluded from totals.
- **Date range guardrails.** A reporting filter must supply a closed `[DateFrom, DateTo]` window where `DateFrom <= DateTo`. Windows wider than 366 days return 400 `REPORT_DATE_RANGE_TOO_WIDE`. As-of-date reports default to branch-local today via `IBranchClock` when omitted.
- **Aggregation primitives go in repositories.** Use cases call typed aggregate queries (`SumActiveByX`, `ListOpenReceivables`, `ListVarianceTimeSeries`) — never materialize whole tables into memory and aggregate in C#.
- **Vertical-slice phases.** Each phase below ships one full feature end-to-end (filter model + projection record + repo aggregate + Infrastructure impl + DTOs + validator + use case + controller + tests + spec extension where applicable). Phase 1 ships only the shared primitives multiple phases consume (`AgingBucket` enum, `ReportAgingBucketizer` static helper, shared resource keys, §6.14 base drop). Every subsequent phase is independently committable and follows the M5/M6 phase-completion convention.
- **Spec sync** is incremental. §6.14 (Reporting Surface) drops in Phase 1 with the base contract; each subsequent phase that introduces a new contract clause bumps the shared `Spec revision` per the sync-group convention.

### Phase 1 — Foundation: shared primitives + §6.14 base drop

Add the small set of primitives more than one later phase consumes. No endpoints. No DI registrations for static helpers.

Pure function — follows the `DueDateCalculator` precedent. Boundary rule: `dueDate > asOfDate` → `Current`; `daysOutstanding ∈ [0, 30]` → `Days0To30`; `[31, 60]` → `Days31To60`; `[61, 90]` → `Days61To90`; `> 90` → `Days91Plus`.

### Phase 2 — Daily Ledger Report

`GET /report/daily-ledger?accountId&dateFrom&dateTo&page&pageSize` — Manager/Admin. The screen managers open first thing every morning: pick an account, pick a date window, see every transaction that hit it plus envelope-level `OpeningBalance` (signed net of `Active` rows with `Date < DateFrom`), `ClosingBalance` (= `OpeningBalance + NetTotal`), and per-direction totals.

Every read `AsNoTracking`, branch-scoped, `Active = true AND Status = Active`-filtered, ordered `Date ASC, CreatedAt ASC, Id ASC`.

### Phase 3 — Fiado Balance Report

`GET /report/fiado/balance?clientId?&asOfDate?` — Manager/Admin. One row per Tab client with their net outstanding per §6.4 (`SUM(Out) − SUM(In)` on `Account.Type = Tab AND Status = Active AND Date <= AsOfDate`). No aging bucket here — that's Phase 4. End-of-day collection-planning view.

- [x] **3.1** Add `FiadoBalanceQuery` to `server.Domain/Models/` (`ClientId?`, `AsOfDate`).
- [x] **3.2** Add `FiadoClientBalanceRow` projection record to `server.Domain/Models/Projections/` (`ClientId`, `ClientName`, `OutstandingTotal`).
- [x] **3.3** Extend `ITransactionsRepository` with `Task<IReadOnlyList<FiadoClientBalanceRow>> ListFiadoBalancesByBranchIdAsNoTracking(branchId, clientId?, asOfDate)` implementing the §6.4 SQL: group by `ClientId` on Tab + Active rows where `Date <= asOfDate`, project `(Direction = Out) ? +Value : -Value` into the sum, exclude zero-balance groups, join `Clients` for `ClientName`, order `ClientName ASC, ClientId ASC`. Branch-scoped, `Active = true AND Status = Active`-filtered.
- [x] **3.4** Add the Infrastructure implementation.
- [x] **3.5** Extend `TransactionsRepositoryBuilder` and add `FiadoBalanceQueryBuilder`.
- [x] **3.6** Add Communication DTOs: `RequestFiadoBalanceJson { Guid? ClientId, DateTime? AsOfDate }`; `ResponseFiadoBalanceJson { DateTime AsOfDate, IReadOnlyList<ResponseFiadoClientBalanceItemJson> Items, decimal TotalOutstanding }`; `ResponseFiadoClientBalanceItemJson { ClientId, ClientName, OutstandingTotal }`. Add `RequestFiadoBalanceJsonBuilder`.
  Note: dropped the `bool Truncated` field. A post-fetch in-memory cap does not protect the database query or aggregation cost, and silently summing only the kept rows would make `TotalOutstanding` ambiguous on a balance report. If branch fiado clientele ever grows past acceptable bounds, prefer real pagination or a repository-level `LIMIT n+1` with an explicit partial-total contract.
- [x] **3.7** Add `FiadoBalanceFluentValidation`: optional `AsOfDate` validity. No pagination (fiado clients per branch are bounded; full ordered grouped set returned).
- [x] **3.8** Implement `GetFiadoBalancesUseCase`: Manager/Admin → validate inline → resolve `asOfDate ?? branchClock.LocalBusinessDate(branchClock.UtcNow())` → call repo (no limit parameter; result is the full ordered set, bounded by branch's active fiado clientele which is typically a few dozen) → compute `TotalOutstanding = sum across the returned items` → return.

Pairs with Phase 3's balance: balance answers "how much does each client owe?", aging answers "of that, which specific rows are how late?". Bumps spec to `v24`.

### Phase 5 — Open-Cheque Aging Report

`GET /report/cheques/open-aging?accountId?&clientId?&asOfDate?&page&pageSize` — Manager/Admin. Multi-row cheque plans viewed as one receivable per `OriginTransactionId`. Each group carries outstanding total, oldest-unpaid date with its bucket, open/total row counts, and the unpaid sibling rows. Without this view, a 6-installment plan shows up as 6 noisy rows in fiado aging — the manager can't see "this client has 4 of 6 cheques outstanding totaling R$ 1,800." Bumps spec to `v25`.

- [x] **5.1** Add `OpenChequeAgingListFilter` (`AccountId?`, `ClientId?`, `AsOfDate`, `Page`, `PageSize`).
- [x] **5.2** Add `OpenChequeGroupRow` projection record (`OriginTransactionId`, `OutstandingTotal`, `OldestOpenDueDate`, `OpenRowCount`, `TotalRowCount`, `AccountId`, `AccountName`, `ClientId?`, `ClientName?`, `Description?`).
- [x] **5.3** Extend `ITransactionsRepository` with `Task<IReadOnlyList<OpenChequeGroupRow>> ListOpenChequeGroupsByBranchIdAsNoTracking(branchId, accountId?, clientId?, page, pageSize)` and `Task<int> CountOpenChequeGroupsByBranchIdAsNoTracking(branchId, accountId?, clientId?)`. Branch-scoped, `Status = Active AND Active = true`. **Explicitly filter `OriginTransactionId IS NOT NULL` before grouping** so ordinary non-installment rows (which have `OriginTransactionId = null`) never collapse into a single bogus group. Then group by `OriginTransactionId`, include only groups with at least one unpaid row (regardless of DueDate so future-due groups reach `Current`). Origin-detail lookup is a separate scoped query (branch + Active + Status filter, no implicit reliance on the group query's scope). Add a new report-specific sibling loader `Task<IReadOnlyList<Transaction>> ListActiveByOriginTransactionIdAndBranchIdAsNoTracking(originId, branchId)` that filters `Active = true AND Status = Active` and orders `DueDate ASC, Id ASC` — do NOT reuse the existing M3 `ListByOriginTransactionIdAndBranchIdAsNoTracking` (which has no Active/Status filter and no ordering; changing it would affect Cancel and other M3 callers).
- [x] **5.4** Add the Infrastructure implementations.

Add the request builder.

The per-group sibling load is N+1 acceptable for typical branch sizes (< 20 open cheque groups); document inline.

### Phase 6 — Installment Plan Preview

`POST /transaction/installment/preview` — non-persisting endpoint that returns the cheque-installment plan rows the system would create, without `AddRange` or `Commit`. Same scope rules as `POST /transaction/installment` (Member with linked operator + account scope, Manager/Admin elevated). Reuses the existing `RequestCreateTransactionInstallmentJson` verbatim. The operator confirmation screen calls this before locking in a multi-row cheque plan; behavior tests + an end-to-end determinism test pin preview/write parity.

Reuses `RequestCreateTransactionInstallmentJson` as-is for the request body.

Run the same flow step-for-step except for persistence: validate inline (`new CreateTransactionInstallmentFluentValidation().Validate(request)`) → `var ctx = await preamble.Resolve(request)` → assert `ctx.TransactionType.SettlementRule == OperatorEnteredCheque` else `ConflictException(TRANSACTION_INSTALLMENT_REQUIRES_CHEQUE)` → `var rows = planBuilder.Build(request, ctx.BranchUser.BranchId, Guid.NewGuid(), ctx.BranchHolidays)` → project rows to `ResponseInstallmentPreviewJson` with `Index = i+1`, `TotalValue = sum`, `InstallmentCount = rows.Count` → return. The use case **does not inject** `ITransactionsRepository` or `IUnitOfWork`; "preview cannot commit" is enforced by construction.

Assert each row's `DueDate`, `Value`, and `Description` match position-for-position between the preview response and the persisted rows. This is the primary preview/write parity gate.

### Phase 7 — Edit Impact Preview

`POST /transaction/{id}/edit-preview?asOfDate?` — Manager/Admin. The safety net for sensitive edits: tells the manager "if you save this change, here's what moves." Returns receivable / fiado-balance / cash-variance deltas without persisting (monthly lock-readiness is deferred to the Milestone 10 report). Reuses `RequestUpdateTransactionJson` verbatim. The impact computation lives in a concrete `TransactionEditImpactProjector` helper — no interface, same convention as `ReportAgingBucketizer` / `InstallmentPlanBuilder` / `TransactionCreatePreamble`, exercised with the real type in tests. Bumps spec to `v26`.

- [x] **7.1** Add `HypotheticalTransactionEdit` value object under `server.Application/UseCases/Transactions/EditPreview/`. Single immutable fields: `TransactionId`, `BranchId`, `AccountId`, `Date`, `AccountType` (cash-variance close coordinates + identity). `Current*`/`Hypothetical*` pairs for every attribute whose divergence the response can already express — `Value`, `Direction` (new pairs) plus `ClientId`, `DueDate`, `PaidAt`, `TransactionTime`, `Description`. `AccountId`/`Date` stay single on purpose: if either became editable the transaction would move between closes, which needs a list-shaped cash-variance response, so splitting them now would imply a capability the single-impact response does not have. The `Hypothetical*` side of `Value`/`Direction` equals the snapshot today (the payload cannot change them), but carrying the pair lets `VarianceDelta` be computed, not assumed. Constructor takes the no-tracking snapshot + the payload and applies the overlay; the snapshot itself is never mutated.

  - `ResponseReceivableImpactJson { AgingBucket? BucketBefore, AgingBucket? BucketAfter, bool RowAppearsInOpenReceivables, bool RowDisappearsFromOpenReceivables }`.
  - `ResponseFiadoBalanceImpactJson { IReadOnlyList<ResponseClientBalanceDeltaJson> Deltas }` plus `ResponseClientBalanceDeltaJson { ClientId, ClientName, OutstandingDelta }`.
  - `ResponseCashVarianceImpactJson { Guid? AccountId, DateTime? Date, DailyCloseStatus? DailyCloseStatus, decimal? CurrentVariance, decimal? ProjectedVariance, decimal VarianceDelta }` — `CurrentVariance`/`ProjectedVariance` populated for any non-`Draft` close (`Submitted`/`Approved`/`Rejected`), null for a `Draft` or absent close; `DailyCloseStatus` surfaces the close state (pending/signed-off/repudiated); `VarianceDelta` is always the computed net-flow delta regardless of close state.
  Reuses `RequestUpdateTransactionJson` from M3 Phase 5 verbatim as the request body — no parallel preview DTO, no field made optional, no "at least one change" rule. `AsOfDate?` lives on the query string (`?asOfDate=YYYY-MM-DD`), not in the body.
- [x] **7.3** Add the concrete `TransactionEditImpactProjector` under `server.Application/Services/Reports/` (no interface — same convention as the other M7 compute helpers; exercised with the real type in tests). Dependencies: `IDailyClosesRepository` (open-close lookup), `IClientsRepository` (fiado client names), `ICashVarianceCalculator` + `ICashVarianceProductResolver` (live variance recompute), and static `ReportAgingBucketizer.BucketFor(...)`. Method signature: `Task<ResponseTransactionEditImpactJson> Project(HypotheticalTransactionEdit edit, DateTime asOfDate, Guid branchId, CancellationToken ct = default)`; `ct` is threaded into every downstream call that accepts one. Computes:
  - `ReceivableImpact`: bucket-before vs bucket-after on `DueDate` shift; `RowAppearsInOpenReceivables` / `RowDisappearsFromOpenReceivables` on `PaidAt` flip.
  - `FiadoBalanceImpact`: deltas only when `AccountType == Tab` and `CurrentClientId != HypotheticalClientId`. Subtract signed `Value` from old client, add to new.
  - `CashVarianceImpact`: look up the daily close for `(BranchId, AccountId, Date)`. `AccountId`/`Date`/`DailyCloseStatus` are populated whenever a close exists. `VarianceDelta` is always computed from net flow before/after (`beforeNetFlow − afterNetFlow`, where `NetFlow = In ? +Value : −Value`) — 0 today because editable fields can't change `Value`/`Direction`, but computed, never hardcoded. `CurrentVariance` is a live recompute via `ICashVarianceCalculator` for **any non-`Draft` close** (`Submitted`/`Approved`/`Rejected` all retain their last-submitted counts), null for a `Draft` close (counts still being entered) or an absent close. The edit itself is gated by the lock date, not the close status, so by here the edit is permitted regardless of status; `DailyCloseStatus` lets the manager tell a pending vs signed-off vs repudiated number. `ProjectedVariance = CurrentVariance + VarianceDelta`, set only when `CurrentVariance` exists. Single-close boundary: this section models exactly one close because `AccountId`/`Date` are immutable; if they ever become editable the response must become a list of cash-variance impacts.
  - Monthly lock-readiness is intentionally **not** computed in this slice — it belongs to the Milestone 10 monthly-reconciliation report. (An earlier real-calc plan added a `MonthlyReconciliationImpact` section + a `HasActiveDraftInBranchAndMonthAsNoTracking` repo method; both were dropped on review — the lock-the-month question is M10's, and an edit can never change it anyway since `Status` is immutable.)

- [x] **7.5** Implement `PreviewEditTransactionUseCase` under `UseCases/Transactions/EditPreview/`. Mirrors `UpdateTransactionUseCase` step-for-step minus `IUnitOfWork` and the mutation/Commit step. Injects exactly: `IAuthenticationService`, `ITransactionsRepository`, `IClientsRepository`, `ITransactionMutationPermissionGuard`, `LockDateGuard`, the concrete `TransactionEditImpactProjector`, `IBranchClock`. `Execute` takes a `CancellationToken` (threaded from the controller's action parameter) and passes it to the projector and to `LockDateGuard.EnsureNotLocked` (which receives the token even though its underlying `ISettingsRepository.GetByBranchIdAsNoTracking` has no token parameter and is not expanded in this slice). **Does not inject** `IUnitOfWork`, `IMemberAccountScopeResolver`, `MemberAccountScopeGuard`, or `TransactionBranchConsistencyService` — the first because preview cannot commit by construction; the next two because preview is Manager/Admin-only (the auth filter blocks Members so the member-scope path is dead code by route protection); the last because the write twin does not use it either. Add a new repo method `Task<Transaction?> GetByIdAndBranchIdAsNoTrackingWithTransactionType(id, branchId)` that mirrors the tracked `GetByIdAndBranchId`'s `.Include(TransactionType).ThenInclude(Category)` but `AsNoTracking()` (it also includes `Account` so the overlay can read `Account.Type`). The cash-variance section adds the repo method `GetByBranchIdAndAccountIdAndDateAsNoTracking` (optional `CancellationToken`). Flow:
  1. Authenticate; assert Manager/Admin role.
  2. Validate inline (`new UpdateTransactionFluentValidation().Validate(request)`).
  3. Load snapshot via the new `GetByIdAndBranchIdAsNoTrackingWithTransactionType` (never the tracked variant) → 404 `TRANSACTION_NOT_FOUND` if missing/cross-branch.
  4. Cancelled → 409 `TRANSACTION_CANNOT_UPDATE_CANCELLED`.
  5. Run `ITransactionMutationPermissionGuard.EnsureAllowed(snapshot, branchUser.Role, linkedOperator: null, utcNow)` (linkedOperator is null because preview is Manager/Admin-only).
  6. Run `LockDateGuard.EnsureNotLocked(branchId, snapshot.Date, TRANSACTION_DATE_LOCKED)`.
  7. If `snapshot.TransactionType.RequiresTabAccountAndClient && request.ClientId is null` → 409 `TRANSACTION_FIADO_REQUIRES_CLIENT`.
  8. If `request.ClientId is { } newClientId`, validate via `IClientsRepository.GetActiveByIdAndBranchIdAsNoTracking(newClientId, branchId)` → 404 `CLIENT_NOT_FOUND` if missing/cross-branch.
  9. Run `UpdateTransactionEntityRelativeValidator.EnsureValid(snapshot, request)` — the same static helper the write twin uses (`DueDate >= Date`, `PaidAt >= Date`).
  10. Build `HypotheticalTransactionEdit` from snapshot + payload.
  11. Resolve `asOfDate ?? branchClock.LocalBusinessDate(branchClock.UtcNow())`.
  12. Call `TransactionEditImpactProjector.Project(edit, asOfDate, branchId, ct)` → return `ResponseEditTransactionPreviewJson`.

  The use case **never** mutates the snapshot. The "preview cannot commit" invariant holds by construction (no `IUnitOfWork` dependency) and is pinned at the architecture level by Phase 12.3.

### Phase 7.1 — Create Transaction Impact Preview

**Status: complete (Slice 7.1 shipped).** A first-class M7 phase that ships after Phase 7 and before the Phase 12 close-out (which audits it). Phase 7's edit-impact preview produces a structurally-zero cash-variance delta because an *update* cannot change `Value`/`Direction`/`Date`/`Account`. A **create** sets all of those fresh, so the same projection produces a genuinely **non-zero** forecast — this phase is where `VarianceDelta`/`ProjectedVariance` on `ResponseCashVarianceImpactJson` earn their keep (0 for edit, real for create), which is the reason to keep those fields on the shared DTO.

`POST /transaction/preview` answers "if I record this new transaction, what moves?" without persisting. Reuses `RequestCreateTransactionJson` verbatim and mirrors `CreateTransactionUseCase`'s validation chain (via `TransactionCreatePreamble`) minus `AddRange`/`Commit`.

**Permission.** Same as `POST /transaction`: `[TokenAuthenticateBranch]`, **not** Manager/Admin-only. The use case must run through `TransactionCreatePreamble`, so Members inherit the same linked-operator and account-scope checks as the real create flow. This keeps preview/write parity: if the caller can create the transaction, they can preview that same create. The response stays scoped to the transaction being previewed — fiado impact returns only the delta for the selected client (not current client balance totals), and cash variance uses only the resolved `(AccountId, Date)` close context and surfaces `DailyCloseStatus`. This is the §6.14 "same scope as the write twin" rule that installment preview already follows; edit impact preview remains the deliberate Manager/Admin exception because it previews an edit to an existing persisted row loaded by id, not a scope-limited create. For Member callers, every impact query must use only the account/client/date resolved by `TransactionCreatePreamble`; do not query branch-wide balances, branch-wide receivables, or all-account variance summaries.

- [x] **7.1.1** Add `HypotheticalTransactionCreate` value object under `server.Application/UseCases/Transactions/CreatePreview/`. Single-valued (a create has no "before"): `AccountId`, `AccountType` (resolved from the account), `Value`, `Direction` (resolved from the type's `Category.DefaultDirection` — **not** in the payload), `Date`, `Status` (`Draft` when `SaveAsDraft`, else `Active`), `ClientId?`, `DueDate` (defaults to `Date` when the payload omits it). `RequestCreateTransactionJson` has **no `PaidAt`**, so a newly created row is always unpaid. Branch scope stays outside the value object and is passed explicitly to the projector. Built from the request + the resolved `TransactionCreatePreamble` context (type/category/account/client).
- [x] **7.1.2** Reuse the Phase 7 impact DTOs verbatim — `ResponseReceivableImpactJson`, `ResponseFiadoBalanceImpactJson` (+ `ResponseClientBalanceDeltaJson`), `ResponseCashVarianceImpactJson`, and the impact envelope (consider renaming `ResponseTransactionEditImpactJson` to a neutral `ResponseTransactionImpactJson` shared by both previews — decide at build time). Add a top-level `ResponseCreateTransactionPreviewJson { Impact, Warnings }` (no `TransactionId` — nothing is created).
- [x] **7.1.3** Add the create impact projection — a concrete helper (no interface; same convention as the edit projector), either a new `TransactionCreateImpactProjector` or a `ProjectCreate(...)` method on the existing one. Reuses `IClientsRepository`, `IDailyClosesRepository`, `ICashVarianceCalculator`, `ICashVarianceProductResolver`, and the static `ReportAgingBucketizer`. **Member-scope leakage guard:** for Member callers, every impact query must use only the account/client/date resolved by `TransactionCreatePreamble`; do not query branch-wide balances, branch-wide receivables, or all-account variance summaries. A `Draft` would-be row has **no Active-filtered impact** (a draft hits none of the §6.12 / open-receivable / fiado sums → all three sections empty/zero). For an `Active` row:
  - `ReceivableImpact`: an unpaid receivable row (Tab account) **appears** — `RowAppearsInOpenReceivables = true`, `BucketAfter = ReportAgingBucketizer.BucketFor(DueDate, asOfDate)`, `BucketBefore = null`. Empty for a non-receivable (e.g. terminal) row.
  - `FiadoBalanceImpact`: a Tab row with a client → a single delta `+signedValue` to the new client (`signedValue = Out ? +Value : −Value`, §6.4). Empty otherwise.
  - `CashVarianceImpact`: **`VarianceDelta = −NetFlow(Direction, Value)`** — the new row adds to its `(account, Date)` `In − Out`, so the variance moves by the negative of its net flow. **Genuinely non-zero** — the whole point of this phase. `CurrentVariance` = live recompute via `ICashVarianceCalculator` when the `(branch, account, Date)` close is **non-Draft** (Submitted/Approved/Rejected — each retains its last-submitted counts; else null), matching Phase 7's rule; `ProjectedVariance = CurrentVariance + VarianceDelta`; surface `DailyCloseStatus` so the caller can see whether the preview touches a pending, signed-off, or repudiated close (e.g. "recording this R$800 on a day whose close is already Submitted moves its variance by −800").
- [x] **7.1.4** Register the projector in DI.
- [x] **7.1.5** Implement `PreviewCreateTransactionUseCase` under `UseCases/Transactions/CreatePreview/`. Mirrors `CreateTransactionUseCase` step-for-step minus `AddRange`/`Commit`. Injects exactly what the write twin injects minus persistence: `IAuthenticationService`, `TransactionCreatePreamble` (which performs the linked-operator + account-scope enforcement, so Members are scoped exactly as in the create flow — **no** separate Manager/Admin role check), the create impact projector, and `IBranchClock`. **Does not inject** `ITransactionsRepository` or `IUnitOfWork` (preview cannot commit by construction). Flow: authenticate the branch user (**any** branch role — the route is `[TokenAuthenticateBranch]`, no Manager/Admin gate) → `new CreateTransactionFluentValidation().Validate(request)` → `var ctx = await preamble.Resolve(request)` (inherits the create flow's Member linked-operator + account-scope 403s, the cross-branch 404s, and the fiado-requires-client / settlement conflicts) → build `HypotheticalTransactionCreate` → resolve `asOfDate ?? branchClock.LocalBusinessDate(branchClock.UtcNow())` → project → return. Thread `CancellationToken`. The use case passes only the preamble-resolved `(account, client, date)` to the projector; for Member callers no impact query may read branch-wide balances, branch-wide receivables, or all-account variance summaries.

Full `[ProducesResponseType]` for 200/400/401/403/404/409 (403 now covers Member out-of-scope / no-linked-operator surfaced by the preamble, not a Manager/Admin role gate).
- [x] **7.1.8** Add `UseCases/Transactions/CreatePreview/` to the **Phase 12.3 preview-never-commits architecture scan** namespace list.

Run the real projector via the repo/calculator builders (no interface to substitute); the preview-never-commits invariant holds by construction (no `IUnitOfWork`/`ITransactionsRepository`).
